50	## Always, before any pipeline
51	
52	1. **Find the file without asking where it is.** Search newest-first:
53	   ```
54	   raw/inbox/   raw/sources/   ~/Downloads/   ~/Desktop/   students/*/transcript-downloads/
55	   ```
56	   Ask only if that comes up empty. He has answered "where is it" too many
57	   times. `raw/inbox/` is the single intake queue; the old top-level
58	   `Clippings/` folder was folded into `raw/sources/clips/` on 2026-09-08, so a
59	   `Clippings/` path in an old note is stale.
60	2. **Dedupe by SHA-256, not by name.** On Windows use `Get-FileHash` or
61	   Python `hashlib`. Check existing archive manifests, source-page metadata,
62	   and dated ingest entries. A matching filename alone is not proof. A matching
63	   hash with completed wiki output means report the existing output and stop.
64	   An archived hash without finished output means resume compilation, not
65	   discard the source. Never delete duplicates.
66	3. **Check the log and source summary.** Record whether the candidate was
67	   previously processed and whether this is a new revision. Preserve both
68	   generations when content changed. A deliberate re-ingest updates the
69	   existing source page with revision provenance, not a duplicate truth.
70	4. **Preserve the input byte-for-byte.** Copy external attachments or inbox
71	   inputs to an appropriate `raw/sources/` archive and verify the SHA-256 before
72	   citing the preserved copy. Record origin, URL/author/date when known,
73	   capture date, archived date, filename, size, and hash. Do not move, delete,
74	   overwrite, or edit original source bytes. Do not follow symlinks outside a
75	   registered source root. Existing archived inputs can be read in place.

81	table above and tell Joshua it was in the wrong place rather than processing it
82	here.
83	
84	1. List `raw/inbox/*.md` newest-first. "The latest" means the newest; a named

86	2. Dedupe (above).
87	3. Read the clip fully. If it is a video page with no transcript, use the

90	   `PROVENANCE.md` and `SHA256SUMS`. Reuse an existing identical archive.
91	   Preserve the inbox original. Processed status comes from its hash, source
92	   page, and log, not from whether the original still exists in the inbox.
93	5. Follow `references/wiki-compilation.md` through the source summary,
94	   relevant existing pages, index update, log entry, and verification.

96	One source at a time unless Joshua explicitly asks for batch work. Discuss

98	file, or ingest with a stated focus authorizes completing that scope; do not
99	introduce an extra confirmation step. Ask when unresolved emphasis, identity,

102	## Transcripts, live
103	
104	Gemini writes "Notes by Gemini" docs to Google Drive after each Meet session.
105	The brain's copy is `students/<slug>/transcript-downloads/YYYY-MM-DD-<slug>-notes-by-gemini.md`
106	(primary record: the brain is its only home). Getting them there is step 1 of
107	`post-session-recap/references/transcript-intake-and-record.md`; follow that section exactly (Calendar sweep by guest email,
108	Drive sweep for unattached docs, export as `text/plain`, resolve the person
109	with `students/_aliases.md`, consultations and unknowns to
110	`students/_other-sessions/`). It is the single source of truth for the pull;
111	do not re-derive the `gws` commands here.
112	
113	Then, per transcript that is new on disk:
114	
115	1. Read the whole transcript, not the Gemini summary at the top. The summary
116	   flattens what the student actually did; the distillation has to name the
117	   answer they picked and the line they misread.
118	2. Append to the student's `teaching-notes.md` (create it from a sibling if the
119	   folder lacks one; it is a standard primary file): what was taught, where they
120	   struggled, what was assigned, what to follow up. Dated heading, append-only,
121	   never regenerate.
122	3. Anything needing an action goes to `open-loops.md` (same rules).
123	4. Add one line to `transcripts.md` so the folder's inventory matches disk.
124	5. Never write to a `*.generated.md` file; those mirror a live backend.
125	6. Append to `wiki/log.md`.
126	
127	Done means every session in the window either has its transcript on disk and
128	distilled, or is listed in the log as "no Gemini doc anywhere" with the date and
129	student, so the next session does not sweep it again.
130	
131	If Joshua requests a session write-up or recap, activate `post-session-recap`
132	and complete its workflow; do not stop at telling him another skill is needed.

143	python system/scripts/transcript-inventory.py              # full report
144	python system/scripts/transcript-inventory.py --unmatched  # only the gaps
145	python system/scripts/transcript-inventory.py --json       # machine-readable
146	python system/scripts/transcript-inventory.py --text "<file>.docx"   # one transcript as text

149	It maps each file to a folder using `students/_aliases.md`, knows that former
150	students live in `students/non-students/`, and prints what it cannot resolve.
151	`--text` exists because this box has no python-docx and no pandoc; it is how a

155	- a name variant of an existing student: add it under `## Aliases` in

157	- a consultation or one-off: add under `## Prospects, no record yet` (the
158	  section the script parses) so it stops surfacing;
159	- a real recurring student with no folder: **ask Joshua before creating one.**
160	  A folder is a judgment about the relationship, not a filing detail.

197	
198	- Never move, delete, or modify original sources. Preserve revisions and hashes.

84	## Ingest compiles one source into the existing knowledge
85	
86	Natural requests such as "ingest this", "add this to my second brain", and "process this article" enter one public workflow. 90	3. Copy evidence into the archive and verify hashes before compiling. Preserve originals. A processed inbox item may be recorded as processed without deletion; moving or deleting needs explicit authorization.

94	7. Update the relevant indexes and reciprocal links. Record contradictory evidence with links to both claims and their dates. Leave the conflict explicitly unresolved until evidence or Joshua adjudicates it; do not silently replace one generation with another.
95	8. Verify edited-page links, evidence paths and hashes, index entries, and the completeness of the intended changes. Append the ingest log entry and report what changed, what remains unresolved, and anything skipped.

**The flow for a clip**

1. **Clip lands.** Obsidian Web Clipper writes a `.md` into `raw/inbox/`. That is the one intake queue.
2. **Find and dedupe.** I look in the inbox, newest first, without asking where the file is. I dedupe by SHA-256, not by filename, and check the archive manifests, `wiki/sources/` and `wiki/log.md`. If the hash matches and the output is finished, I report it and stop. If the hash is archived but compilation never finished, I resume.
3. **Archive.** I copy the clip byte-for-byte into `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` with `PROVENANCE.md` and `SHA256SUMS`, then verify the hash. The inbox original stays, so a clip sitting in the inbox doesn't tell you whether it's been processed.
4. **Read.** I read the whole clip. A video clip goes through `watch` for a transcript, because a thumbnail isn't evidence. Images get inspected separately.
5. **Takeaways.** I present the key takeaways and the pages they'd touch. If you say "ingest this" or "implement this", that authorizes the whole job and I don't add a confirmation step.
6. **Compile.** I write a source summary in `wiki/sources/`: identity, hash link, what the source claims, then my interpretation kept separate. I then update the existing pages it affects, and search before creating anything. AI and Claude/Codex material routes through `references/ai-workspace/index.md`. LSAT material goes to `corpus/`. Student material goes to `students/<name>/`.
7. **Index, log, verify.** I update `wiki/index.md` (`py system/scripts/wiki-maintenance.py --index`), append an `## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title` entry to `wiki/log.md`, and check links and hashes. `--lint` is available for a structural check.

I do one source at a time unless you ask for a batch. A source's claims are dated claims, not verified facts, until I check them against official docs or your actual setup. Live systems like Cal.com, Supabase and Gmail win over anything the brain says about operational state.

- **Possible duplicates:** `Dashboard.md`, `Dashboard 1.md` and `Dashboard 2.md` look like duplicates or revisions. The same goes for `Claude Now Does Video...` and its "1" copy. A hash check would settle both.

11	# Turn AI clips into useful workspace knowledge
12	
13	Joshua expects a substantial share of incoming clips to cover Claude, Codex, AI tips, prompts, repositories, new skills, product updates, and possible workspace improvements. This is the navigation and filing guide for that material. It is separate from the [LSAT teaching corpus](../../corpus/index.md), while remaining part of the same second brain.
14	
15	This hub organizes existing authoritative pages rather than copying their knowledge into another set of pages. The intake rules remain in the [LLM Wiki schema](../../system/schema/llm-wiki.md), and the overall catalog remains [the wiki index](../../wiki/index.md).
16	
17	[Recovered practical methods](migrated-source-methods.md) add the old vault's detailed artifact-review, skill-evaluation and evidence-checking recipes, plus its unimplemented annotation idea. [Migration coverage](../../research/obsidian-vault-comparison/2026-10-07/active-migration-coverage.md) maps the original research and root files to current homes and explains exclusions.
18	
19	[Karpathy source compilation](../../wiki/karpathy/index.md) now spans domain source pages and explicit semantic coverage. [Use the teaching setup](karpathy-method.md) for the reviewed rules, project agent, teaching skill and scoped Python execution gate. Compilation of the saved collection is complete according to the [current compilation audit](../../research/karpathy-brain-2026-10-08/COMPILATION-AUDIT.md). This does not establish exhaustive public-source acquisition or full understanding of binary, generated or media payloads. The [verified collection handoff](../../research/karpathy-brain-2026-10-08/HANDOFF-VERIFICATION.md) records missing coverage, attribution limits and corrections to the original completeness claims.
20	
21	[Resumable X collector](x-archive-collector.md) documents the free local script, signed-in persistent Chrome route, evidence ledgers, source-preserving repairs and retrieval limits.
22	
23	## Find the right kind of AI knowledge
24	
25	| Category | What belongs here | Existing home to consult or update |
26	|---|---|---|
27	| Claude and Codex workflows | Build/review handoffs, work briefs, tool orchestration, runtime differences | [Claude plus Codex](../claude-plus-codex.md) |
28	| Skills and reusable prompts | Skill evaluation, reusable instructions, activation rules, deciding whether an existing skill already covers the job | [Skill catalog](../../skills/INDEX.md), [declared runtime differences](../../skills/PARITY.md), [operating rules](../../AGENTS.md), and [Three Ms framework](../3ms-framework.md) |
29	| Tools, repositories, and integrations | What a tool could solve, prerequisites, overlaps, costs, and evidence from testing | [Obsidian command center](../obsidian-command-center.md), [Firecrawl methods](../firecrawl.md), or the existing reference for the particular tool |
30	| Tips, insights, and prompting methods | Useful reasoning patterns, context management, evaluating outputs, and methods that survive product changes | [Three Ms framework](../3ms-framework.md), [second-brain architecture](../second-brain-levels.md), and [website planning](../website-design-planning.md) when relevant |
31	| Product updates | Claimed new capabilities, affected runtime/version, official evidence, availability, and practical consequences | Update the existing affected reference. Keep release evidence in its source summary rather than creating an undated universal claim |
32	| Proposed improvements | A concrete gap, expected benefit, alternatives, and a testable next action | [Automation backlog](../automation-backlog.md) for automation proposals; [work ledger](../../wiki/tasks.md) for agreed work |
33	| Cloud, local, and backup setup | What exists in each runtime, snapshot freshness, exclusions, and restore limits | [Local-to-cloud sync setup](../../setup/github-sync/README.md), [backup setup](../../setup/backup/README.md), and [application cloud setup](../cloud-session-setup.md) for its specifically stated application scope |
34	| Graphs and navigation | Retrieval problems, graph use, and source-to-graph provenance | [Second-brain architecture](../second-brain-levels.md), [Graphify bridge](../graphify-obsidian-bridge.md), and [Obsidian command center](../obsidian-command-center.md) |
35	
36	These links establish where to retrieve and maintain a subject. They do not certify every dated claim on the linked pages as current. Check the affected environment before relying on setup or availability claims.
37	
38	New durable AI subjects with no existing authoritative home can receive a descriptive page under `references/ai-workspace/`. Create that page only when material exists to support it, then link it here and from the wiki index. Categories are navigation, not empty folders that every clip must fill.
39	
40	## A clip follows one intake path
41	
42	1. **Capture:** the intended Web Clipper destination is `raw/inbox/`. Saving a clip does not run ingestion, install a skill, or update a configuration. The browser's selected vault/template must actually point there; this document does not change browser settings.
43	2. **Request:** Joshua asks to ingest a source, or explicitly authorizes a batch. Determine whether it concerns AI operations, LSAT teaching, a student, or another domain.
44	3. **Preserve:** check content hashes and prior processing. Copy original evidence into `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/`, verify its bytes, and record provenance. Missing images, transcripts, or inaccessible repository contents remain explicit gaps. Preserve changed generations separately.
45	4. **Summarize:** create or update the source's page in `wiki/sources/`, with source identity, dates, archived evidence, claims, limitations, and affected knowledge pages.
46	5. **Compile:** compare the source with current knowledge and the actual setup. Update the owning reference instead of making a parallel truth. AI operating methods stay outside the LSAT corpus. A mixed source can inform both domains through distinct updates and shared evidence links.
47	6. **Connect and check:** update this hub when navigation changes, the complete wiki catalog, reciprocal links where useful, and the dated wiki log. Check links, contradictions, and evidence before reporting completion.
48	7. **Implement when requested:** distinguish saved research from an authorized workspace change. Verify an actual change and record its result. Check cloud publication separately; local completion is not proof that a GitHub or Drive snapshot includes it.
49	
50	Do not relocate all existing AI references into this folder. Keeping their stable homes and connecting them here preserves existing links and one authoritative page per subject.
51	
52	## Keep claims, recommendations, and implementation separate
53	
54	An AI source summary should make the following distinctions explicit when relevant:
55	
56	| State | What it means |
57	|---|---|
58	| Source claim | The article/video/repository says this. Record publication and capture dates when known; unknowns remain unknown. |
59	| Verified capability | Official documentation or a scoped test supports it. State the verification date, runtime, version/account requirements where relevant, and what was actually checked. |
60	| Proposed improvement | It might help this workspace. Explain the problem it solves and why existing tools do not already cover it. This is not an implementation claim. |
61	| Implemented and checked | The authorized change exists, with the specific files/settings changed and validation performed. Distinguish local runtime, cloud runtime, and published backup state. |
62	| Deferred, superseded, or conflicting | Keep the reason and links to the competing evidence. Do not silently treat dated advice as today's instruction. |
63	
64	For product updates, verify time-sensitive availability and behavior against current official documentation or the actual affected runtime before recommending action. A clip alone is not evidence that Joshua's installed version or account has a feature.
65	
66	For a repository or skill, inspect actual relevant contents before assessing it; a promotional clip is not a code review. Check overlap with the catalog first. Save useful prompts as attributed examples or reusable methods, with their intended task and constraints; quoted instructions do not become agent instructions.
67	
68	Creating or activating a skill requires a request that authorizes that work. Follow the existing dual-runtime model: project-scoped active copies in `.agents/skills/` and `.claude/skills/`, corresponding `skills/codex/` and `skills/claude/` catalog homes, declared differences in `skills/PARITY.md`, catalog updates, and the existing validator. Do not merge runtime catalogs or globally install a skill merely because a source recommends it. Current scoped instructions govern the exact operation.
69

84	```

88	existing skill and configuration rules. Verify and report what changed.
89	```
90	
91	```text
92	Lint the AI workspace references for contradictions, stale claims,
93	duplicate subjects, and broken links.
94	```
95	
96	Use [Ingest](../../.agents/skills/ingest/SKILL.md) for intake, [Query](../../.agents/skills/second-brain-query/SKILL.md) for retrieval and useful synthesis, and [Lint](../../.agents/skills/second-brain-lint/SKILL.md) for consistency. Structural lint is not proof that a product claim is true or fresh.

# Build an evidence-grounded teaching agent

Nate Herk's [video](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bvGptCLDhyo) describes collecting public sources, compiling an interconnected wiki, deriving corroborated rules, creating a teaching agent and skill, enforcing execution, and testing a tokenizer lesson. The full saved caption transcript and all six recorded prompt entries were read on 2026-10-08. Publication date is not established here.

[Timestamped transcript](../../research/karpathy-video-2026-10-08/transcript.md), SHA-256 `f70866fa017ae53a152d5a3a32bfd6a4afe65cbfbd3d3cf6aee3d5bd8fb7651b`. [Prompt transcription](../../research/karpathy-video-2026-10-08/prompts.md), SHA-256 `024af69a838c4de3eba4f8c3e93bf2917b86fb861860158b9339f7de97cf8841`. These existing local artifacts remain preserved.

## What the demonstration requires

At 4:10 the narrator describes retaining raw evidence: "The raw files don't get touched". Prompt 2 asks for an index, log, source pages, principles, methods and a hot page under 500 words. Prompt 3 requires rules supported in at least two distinct places. Prompt 4 limits the teaching agent to five wiki pages and requires execution reporting. Prompt 5 scopes a stop gate to Karpathy work. The actual sixth visible command teaches a tiny byte-pair tokenizer; a separate numbered sixth card was not verified.

## Application here

The existing [Karpathy wiki](../karpathy/index.md) owns compiled knowledge. [Execution status](../../research/karpathy-brain-2026-10-08/VIDEO-PROMPT-EXECUTION.md) distinguishes finished stages from gaps. Our browser collection substitutes for the paid X API. Claude and Codex implementations follow their own runtime formats and the existing vault schema. Joshua's identity and teaching voice remain authoritative.

The narrator's seven labels are hypotheses until direct Karpathy sources corroborate them. Captions can contain errors, and the video's biography is not independently verified here. A working local demonstration does not establish production acceptance or whole-corpus coverage.

97 wiki/VIDEO-GENERATION-OPTIONS-2026-09-09.md
# Making videos from Claude Code: the options, priced

Date: 2026-09-09. Method: five Firecrawl `agent` jobs (spark-1-pro) plus two
developer-index searches. All figures are from official pricing/licence pages
retrieved on this date and are cited below. Related: [[tasks]], [[hot]].

## The recommendation

**Remotion, driven by Claude Code through Remotion's own agent skills.** For a
solo operator it is free, including commercial use and automated local
rendering, so it costs nothing beyond the Claude Code membership. That was the
constraint, and it is the only serious option that meets it.

Install path:

```bash
npx create-video@latest
npx remotion skills add
```

There is also an official Claude Code plugin: `remotion-dev/claude-code-plugin`.
The older official Remotion MCP is **deprecated** — the docs say not to use it
for new installs. Skills/plugin is the current path.

## Why Remotion and not the alternatives

| Option | Licence | Cost for a solo operator | LLM fit |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Remotion** 4.0.523 | Source-available, proprietary | **Free** (orgs ≤3 people, all features, unlimited commercial use) | Excellent — official Claude Code skills + plugin |
| Revideo 0.11.0 | MIT | Free | Good, plain TypeScript, but no narration layer |
| Manim / ManimGL | MIT | Free | Good for math/diagram animation only |

The Remotion licence has a trap worth knowing even though it does not bite you:
organizations of **4 or more people** need a Company License, and the
"Automators" tier ($0.01/render, **$100/month minimum**) covers batch rendering,
prompt-to-video apps, and automated video products. At your size the Free
License covers all of it. If Aspiring Attorneys ever passes three people, that
changes — it is a headcount trigger, not a usage trigger. Verify the terms
yourself before relying on it commercially; this is a licence, not a spec.

## Working pipelines other people have built

- **`runesleo/claude-video-kit`** (MIT, 119 stars) — closest to your use case. A
  JSON script becomes a 1080x1920 explainer MP4 via one `render.sh`: TTS,
  Whisper-aligned captions, Remotion slides.
- **`Bhonar/docs-to-video`** — MCP extracts a page, a Claude Code skill writes the
  scenes, ElevenLabs narrates with real word timestamps, Remotion renders.
  Strongest repository evidence of the four.
- **`Agents365-ai/video-podcast-maker`** (MIT, 1.6k stars) — script-driven 4K
  narrated video, local Edge/Azure TTS, CI smoke-render.
- **`itsjwill/vanta`** (MIT) — voice cloning + WhisperX word-accurate captions.
- **`Yusuke710/manim-skill`** — Claude Code skill: plan, write Manim, render,
  watch it, iterate.

## The narration question

You do not need a TTS vendor. `model_runs` already holds **714 recordings of you
doing the drill**, 713 transcribed with word timings. For RRC videos the
narration is already recorded, in your own voice, and Remotion can lay
transcript-timed captions against it.

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\wiki\\sources\\claude-now-does-video-free-javascript.md", "content": "---\ntype: source\nsubject: Chase AI video, Claude makes videos with JavaScript\ncreated: 2026-10-10\nupdated: 2026-10-10\naliases: [Claude Now Does Video, JavaScript video pipeline]\ntags: [ai-workspace, claude, video, playwright, ffmpeg]\nsources: [../../raw/sources/clips/claude-now-does-video-free-javascript/PROVENANCE.md]\n---\n\n# Claude writes the video as a function of time, then checks its own frames\n\nChase AI's video \"Claude Now Does Video (FOR FREE) Thanks To JavaScript\" ([YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=rscb1DgJtNg)) argues that Claude Code can produce finished MP4s with plain JavaScript, with no Remotion, no After Effects and no AI video generator. The full clip transcript was read on 2026-10-10 (13:56 of video, 2,902 words). Publication date is unknown.\n\nEvidence: [archived clip, provenance and hashes](../../raw/sources/clips/claude-now-does-video-free-javascript/PROVENANCE.md). Archived clip SHA-256 `8772757fc82f20e0ef92126026444ac6d2bc115821e61ad6c8805998d85d075a`. A second, rougher caption generation of the same video (`d5bb8d0e…`) remains in `raw/inbox/` and was deliberately not archived or compiled; the provenance file records both.\n\n## What the video claims\n\nAll of this is the narrator's account, not verified here.\n\n**The mechanism (0:23 to 2:55).** Claude writes a function that takes a time `t` and returns one picture. The same `t` always gives the same picture, so any frame can be redrawn or checked alone. A headless browser draws 60 frames per second, FFmpeg stitches them into an MP4, and sound is also a function of `t`. The narrator says Claude cannot watch a video but can look at the frames, spot what is wrong, and fix the function. His demonstration video was a one-shot attempt in the style of a Three Blue One Brown explainer, with ElevenLabs narration.\n\n**Level 1, prompting (4:10 to 7:11).** He gave Opus 5.5 one prompt for a 15-second motion-graphics showreel with no skills, at low, high and \"ultra code\" effort. He reports roughly 20 minutes, 30 minutes and 8 hours, with all three \"pretty solid\" and ultra best. His conclusion is that weak results are usually a prompt problem, not an effort problem. The one change he recommends is asking for a storyboard first: the scenes are shown as frames before any code runs, which avoids long regenerate loops.\n\n**Level 2, tools and looping (7:11 to 9:35).** The prompt should state length, aspect ratio (full screen or vertical), JavaScript, the soundtrack route, and \"render to MP4 with Playwright and FFmpeg\". It should end with a check instruction: before calling it done, pull a contact sheet with FFmpeg, look at it, fix what is wrong, re-render, repeat. Audio can come first (supply a narration MP3 and have visuals timed to it) or visuals first. He says Opus 5.5 and Fable 5.1 often do the check unprompted. He prefers ElevenLabs for narration (about five dollars a month, not sponsored) and says open-source narration also exists.\n\n**Level 3, references (9:35 to 11:35).** Give an image to copy a style, or a video to borrow composition. His example fed Claude an Opus 5.5 plus Remotion video about currency and got a JavaScript recreation about the history of electricity, \"from the same family of design\". Inspiration sources named: skillery.dev (an AI-video category; paid, partly free) and a Twitter creator he calls Kevin.\n\n**Level 4, a skill (11:43 to 13:26).** He packaged the process as a Claude Code skill with seven preloaded styles (cut paper, crosshatch, Rizzoli sketchbook, isometric, and others he does not name in the captions) and custom styles from a reference. Stages: intake questions (subject, format, audio, optional hero character), story, storyboard, then code and delivery, with the Playwright and FFmpeg loop automatic. It installs as a plugin or by handing Claude Code a URL, and it checks prerequisites including Playwright, FFmpeg and faster-whisper, and offers the ElevenLabs connector. He calls it a work in progress and invites pull requests.\n\n**Cost claim.** \"It's all code. It's essentially free.\" The only cost is Claude usage, plus ElevenLabs if used. The video's closing sponsor segment is for his own paid community.\n\n## Limitations of this source\n\n- **Captions, not script.** Names and numbers are heard text. \"Ultra code\", \"Fable 5.1\", \"skillery.dev\" and the effort timings should be checked against the video before anyone quotes them.\n- **No skill identity.** The URL for the skill is not in the clip, so its name, repository, licence and contents are unknown. Nothing here lets us assess or install it.\n- **Description mismatch.** The clipped blurb belongs to a different Chase AI video; only its timestamps match. See the provenance file.\n- **Promotional frame.** The video ends with and opens a sponsor segment for the narrator's paid course and \"AI OS\". \"Biggest upgrade\" style framing is the channel's, not an independent finding.\n- **No evidence shown for the cost and speed claims** beyond his own runs. Eight hours for the best result is a stated cost, not a free one in time.\n\n## How it fits this workspace\n\nThis is a dated source claim, captured and read, not yet evaluated or tested here. Status: **captured**.\n\nIt does not conflict with the earlier recommendation in [video generation options](../VIDEO-GENERATION-OPTIONS-2026-09-09.md); it adds a second route. That page recommends Remotion driven by Claude Code skills, free for organizations of three or fewer. This video's route drops the framework and renders from plain JavaScript through a headless browser and FFmpeg, and the narrator's own reference video was made with Remotion, so the two are complementary rather than rival. See the dated section added to that page for the comparison and the open tests.\n\nLocal prerequisites checked on 2026-10-10 from existing records: FFmpeg is installed ([toolchain](../../references/ai-workspace/windows-cli-toolchain.md)). Playwright and faster-whisper were not checked and are not recorded as installed.\n\nRelated: [AI workspace hub](../../references/ai-workspace/index.md), [video generation options](../VIDEO-GENERATION-OPTIONS-2026-09-09.md).\n"}

56:## Herdr makes the tandem workflow visible and persistent
58:Herdr is the terminal and session layer around Claude Code and Codex. It does not replace either agent or the shared-file architecture. It gives the workflow an operational surface:
64:- **Persistence** keeps pane processes running after the Herdr client detaches or its terminal window closes. Reopen `herdr` to reconnect. Use `herdr server stop` only when the processes should actually stop.
65:- **Agent control** lets an agent with the Herdr skill inspect neighboring panes, split panes, run commands, read output, and wait for another agent.

54	**Session handoff** (Nate's own skill) is the bridge: summarize decisions / active files / next steps, paste into the other tool, keep going. When one gets stuck, hand off and the other often unsticks it in seconds.
55	
56	## Herdr makes the tandem workflow visible and persistent
57	
58	Herdr is the terminal and session layer around Claude Code and Codex. It does not replace either agent or the shared-file architecture. It gives the workflow an operational surface:
59	
60	- **Workspaces** separate projects.
61	- **Tabs** separate activities within a project.
62	- **Panes** show Claude Code, Codex, servers, tests, and helper agents side by side.
63	- **Agent status** rolls up which pane is working, idle, or blocked.
64	- **Persistence** keeps pane processes running after the Herdr client detaches or its terminal window closes. Reopen `herdr` to reconnect. Use `herdr server stop` only when the processes should actually stop.
65	- **Agent control** lets an agent with the Herdr skill inspect neighboring panes, split panes, run commands, read output, and wait for another agent.
66

\# Herdr agent guide

Use this guide to help a human understand, set up, or troubleshoot Herdr. It covers Herdr's concept model, setup path, and diagnosis recipes. Canonical documentation lives at https://herdr.dev/docs/. Point the human there for more detail, and verify any command you are unsure about against those pages instead of guessing.

If you are running \*inside\* a Herdr pane (the environment variable \`HERDR\_ENV=1\` is set), Herdr also ships a skill file that teaches you to control Herdr through the \`herdr\` CLI: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/herdrdev/herdr/master/skills/herdr/SKILL.md. That file teaches you to operate Herdr; this one teaches you to guide a human.

\## What Herdr is

Herdr is a terminal workspace manager for AI coding agents. Like tmux, it is a multiplexer: a background server owns real terminal processes, and clients attach to render them. Panes keep running when the human detaches, closes the terminal, or disconnects SSH.

Unlike tmux, Herdr is mouse-first and agent-aware. The whole UI is clickable — panes, tabs, workspaces, split borders, right-click menus. Herdr detects coding agents running inside panes and shows each one's state in a sidebar, so the human can see across all their projects which agent is \`working\`, which is \`blocked\` waiting for input, and which is \`done\`. A CLI and a local socket API let scripts and agents drive Herdr programmatically.

\## Concept model

Teach these in this order:

\- \*\*Session\*\* — a persistent background server namespace. Running \`herdr\` attaches to the default session. Named sessions (\`herdr session attach work\`) are fully separate runtime namespaces; most people only need the default.
\- \*\*Workspace\*\* — the project-level container. One per repo, task, or investigation. Owns tabs and panes. The sidebar rolls agent states up per workspace.
\- \*\*Tab\*\* — a layout inside a workspace, for separating views like \`agents\`, \`logs\`, \`server\`.
\- \*\*Pane\*\* — a real terminal. Splittable right or down. Survives client detach.
\- \*\*Agent\*\* — a process Herdr recognizes inside a pane. States: \`working\`, \`blocked\`, \`done\`, \`idle\`, \`unknown\`.
\- \*\*Modes\*\* — terminal mode sends keys to the focused pane; prefix mode (\`ctrl+b\`, then one action key) sends one command to Herdr; navigate mode is a persistent navigation surface.

Full concepts page: https://herdr.dev/docs/concepts/

\## Install

Linux and macOS:

\`\`\`bash
curl -fsSL https://herdr.dev/install.sh \| sh
herdr
\`\`\`

Windows PowerShell:

\`\`\`powershell
powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -c "irm https://herdr.dev/install.ps1 \| iex"
herdr
\`\`\`

If endpoint security blocks that fileless PowerShell command, use Command Prompt:

\`\`\`cmd
curl.exe -fsSLo install.cmd https://herdr.dev/install.cmd && install.cmd && del install.cmd
herdr
\`\`\`

Homebrew, mise, and Nix installs, verification, and manual downloads: https://herdr.dev/docs/install/. Direct installs use the stable channel by default and update with \`herdr update\`; preview is opt-in. Package-manager installs update through that package manager. Check the version with \`herdr --version\`.

\## First-run walkthrough

Check your environment first. If \`HERDR\_ENV=1\` is set, you are already running inside a Herdr pane. The human is already attached, so skip step 1 and never tell them to run \`herdr\` from your pane; Herdr blocks nested launches by design. Start with step 2, and consider the skill file below.

Walk the human through this sequence:

1\. \`cd\` into a project and run \`herdr\`. It launches or attaches to the default background session and creates a workspace automatically. First run shows an onboarding flow.
2\. Start their coding agent in the pane — \`claude\`, \`codex\`, or any supported agent (full list: https://herdr.dev/docs/agents/). Herdr detects it automatically; the sidebar shows its state. Install the matching integration when available. Depending on the agent, it provides lifecycle state, native session restore, or both. For example, \`herdr integration install claude\` adds native session restore, while Claude's state still comes from screen detection.
3\. Start with the mouse: click panes and tabs to focus, drag split borders, right-click for menus, drag-select to copy. No keybindings are required to use Herdr.
4\. Split panes: right-click menu, or \`prefix+v\` (right) / \`prefix+minus\` (down). New tab: \`prefix+c\`.
5\. Detach with \`prefix+q\` (press \`ctrl+b\`, release, press \`q\`) or close the terminal window. Everything keeps running. Reattach later with \`herdr\`.
6\. To actually stop everything: \`herdr server stop\`.

\## The keyboard story

New users do not need to learn keybindings; the mouse covers everything. When the human wants keyboard control:

\- The prefix key is \`ctrl+b\` by default. \`prefix+?\` shows every active binding live.
\- The guided keyboard page covers the prefix, the bindings to learn first, and a vetted prefix-free setup using \`ctrl+alt\` chords: https://herdr.dev/docs/keyboard/. Recommend it over improvising.
\- Every binding, including the prefix itself, is configurable under \`\[keys\]\` in the config file.
\- If a direct chord does nothing, the OS or the outer terminal consumed it before Herdr could see it. The keyboard page explains which chords are safe and why.

\## Install the Herdr skill into yourself

Herdr ships \`skills/herdr/SKILL.md\` (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/herdrdev/herdr/master/skills/herdr/SKILL.md), which teaches a coding agent to control Herdr from inside a pane: splitting panes, running commands without stealing focus, reading output, and waiting on other agents.

Once the human is set up, offer to install it for your coding agent so future sessions can control Herdr directly. For agents supported by the open skills CLI, use \`npx skills add herdrdev/herdr --skill herdr -g\`. For agents without a skill system, add the GitHub copy above to their global custom instructions. Ask the human before writing to their config locations, and use the GitHub copy above as the source of truth.

\## Configuration

\- Config file: \`~/.config/herdr/config.toml\` on Linux and macOS; \`%APPDATA%\\herdr\\config.toml\` on Windows. Herdr works without one.
\- Print the full default config: \`herdr --default-config\`.
\- Apply edits to a running server: \`herdr server reload-config\` (or the global menu → reload config).
\- Main areas: \`\[keys\]\` keybindings, \`\[theme\]\` themes, \`\[ui\]\` sidebar and UI behavior, \`\[terminal\]\` shell defaults, \`\[update\]\` channel.
\- Full reference: https://herdr.dev/docs/configuration/

\## Diagnosis recipes

\- \*\*Agent not detected or wrong state:\*\* Run \`herdr agent list\` to see what Herdr sees and \`herdr agent explain  --json\` to see why the detector classified a pane that way. Integrations can provide lifecycle state, native session restore, or both; check \`herdr integration status\` and the agent support table before assuming an integration replaces screen detection. Details: https://herdr.dev/docs/agents/ and https://herdr.dev/docs/integrations/
\- \*\*A keybinding does nothing:\*\* the outer terminal or desktop environment owns that chord. Point the human to https://herdr.dev/docs/keyboard/ to pick a safe one or free the chord in their terminal settings.
\- \*\*Something looks wrong at startup or with the socket API:\*\* Default-session logs live in \`~/.config/herdr/\` on Linux and macOS and \`%APPDATA%\\herdr\\\` on Windows. Named-session logs live under \`sessions//\` inside that directory. \`herdr status\`, \`herdr status server\`, and \`herdr status client\` summarize the runtime.
\- \*\*Remote use:\*\* SSH to the machine and run \`herdr\` there (works like tmux), or attach as a thin local client with \`herdr --remote \`. Trade-offs: https://herdr.dev/docs/how-to-work/
\- \*\*What survives a detach, restart, or update:\*\* https://herdr.dev/docs/session-state/

\## Rules for you

\- Do not invent keybindings, config keys, or CLI flags. The ones in this file are accurate as of writing; for anything else, read the linked docs page first.
\- Teach mouse before keyboard for humans new to multiplexers.
\- Herdr is not tmux: do not give tmux commands, tmux config syntax, or \`.tmux.conf\` advice for Herdr questions.
\- For automation, scripting, or controlling Herdr from code, point to the CLI reference (https://herdr.dev/docs/cli-reference/) and socket API (https://herdr.dev/docs/socket-api/).

> `herdr machine list` lists saved connection profiles, not a cross-machine pane inventory; add `--json` for scripts. Only add, remove, enable, or disable profiles when the user asks. Use `herdr machine status [<label-or-id>] --json` to diagnose a failing machine. `herdr machine reconnect` needs the user to complete SSH authentication in their terminal; do not run it for them. name: codex-model-roles
description: "Which Codex model does what — gpt-6-astra designs, specs and reviews; gpt-6-luna high builds; a 400 on sol or luna means a stale CLI, use dev/tools/node/codex."
metadata:

Codex model by role (Joshua, 2026-09-23):
- **Design, spec writing, plan review** (`/codex-review`, design critique): `gpt-6-astra` (he picked medium effort for plan review).
- **Building code** (`/codex-build`, any Codex implementation run): `gpt-6-luna` at `high` reasoning effort. Never astra.
- The config default (`gpt-6-luna`, low) is neither; always pass `-m` and `-c model_reasoning_effort=` explicitly and echo them before launch.

- **Fixed 2026-09-23:** a 400 "not supported when using Codex with a ChatGPT account" on sol/luna meant an outdated CLI, not the login. The stale 0.153.4 in `AppData\Roaming\npm` sits first on the bash PATH; the current CLI is `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\tools\node\codex` (0.156.1), where sol and luna both run on the ChatGPT login. Call it by full path. Joshua does not want an API-key login and picked `gpt-6-luna` high for builds (never luna low).

**Why:** building from a frozen spec does not need the strongest model; astra's value is in thinking through design and specs. He objected when astra built Classroom Home phase 1 (PR #133).

**How to apply:** on every Codex launch, choose the model by role, state it before running. Related: [[firecrawl-via-cli-not-mcp]] (same "use the cheaper path when it does the job" instinct).

11	Codex model by role (Joshua, 2026-09-23):
12	- **Design, spec writing, plan review** (`/codex-review`, design critique): `gpt-6-astra` (he picked medium effort for plan review).
13	- **Building code** (`/codex-build`, any Codex implementation run): `gpt-6-luna` at `high` reasoning effort. Never astra.
14	- The config default (`gpt-6-luna`, low) is neither; always pass `-m` and `-c model_reasoning_effort=` explicitly and echo them before launch.
15	
16	- **Fixed 2026-09-23:** a 400 "not supported when using Codex with a ChatGPT account" on sol/luna meant an outdated CLI, not the login. The stale 0.153.4 in `AppData\Roaming\npm` sits first on the bash PATH; the current CLI is `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\tools\node\codex` (0.156.1), where sol and luna both run on the ChatGPT login. Call it by full path. Joshua does not want an API-key login and picked `gpt-6-luna` high for builds (never luna low).
17	
18	**Why:** building from a frozen spec does not need the strongest model; astra's value is in thinking through design and specs. He objected when astra built Classroom Home phase 1 (PR #133).
19	
20	**How to apply:** on every Codex launch, choose the model by role, state it before running. Related: [[firecrawl-via-cli-not-mcp]] (same "use the cheaper path when it does the job" instinct).

The full clip transcript was read on 2026-10-10 (about 10:18 of video). Herdr's repository is linked in the description as `github.com/ogulcancelik/herdr`; the captions mis-hear the name as \"Herder\" and \"Hurder\".\n\nEvidence: [archived clip, provenance and hashes](../../raw/sources/clips/herdr-claude-codex-headache/PROVENANCE.md). Archived clip SHA-256 `44d232646d6de038bfeecc4e2496f01b60ce16b305b87b377fcfb7b8a8f47d2e`.\n\n**This video was already ingested on 2026-09-22.** That ingest updated [Claude and Codex on one project](../../references/claude-plus-codex.md) and installed Herdr, but wrote no source summary and archived no clip. This page is the missing source summary, written from a newer caption generation. The provenance file lists all three clip copies and records an unresolved hash discrepancy for the 2026-09-22 file.\n\n## What the video claims\n\nAll of this is the narrator's account, checked here only where noted.\n\n- **Three benefits (0:31).** Organization (one window instead of many loose terminals), monitoring (a sidebar showing which agents are working, done or stuck), and persistence (Herdr runs as a server, so closing the window does not stop the agents; reopen and they are still running).\n- **Versus tmux and Cmux (1:43 to 2:17).** The narrator says tmux does not show agent status out of the box, and that Cmux lacks persistence and is hard to use on Windows. He also says a running session can be reached from a phone or over SSH.\n- **Install (2:33).** One line from `herdr.dev` or the GitHub page, then type `herdr`.\n- **Structure (2:56 to 5:37).** Spaces are projects, tabs are subtasks inside a space, and panes hold the agents. Everything is mouse-clickable, not only keybinds.\n- **Claude and Codex together (5:37 to 8:14).** His main use is making visible what is normally done headlessly: a skill where Claude plans, Codex critiques, and they iterate. With the Herdr skill he has Claude open a pane that shows that exchange live.\n- **The Herdr skill (6:44).** It teaches an agent to create panes and spaces itself. One line installs it, and he mentions a plugin marketplace for add-ons.\n- **Persistence demo (8:14).** He closes the terminal completely mid-task, relaunches with `herdr`, and both agents are still working. He says this survives as long as the computer stays on.\n- **Keybinds (9:05).** Tmux-like: `Ctrl+B` for prefix mode, then a key (for example `V` to split vertically).\n- **Scope claim (9:47).** Claude Code's own agent view works only inside Claude Code; Herdr covers any harness, including Codex and OpenCode.\n\n## What was checked against reality\n\nChecked 2026-10-10 against Herdr's own CLI help, its published agent guide (`herdr.dev/agent-guide.md`) and the GitHub releases API:\n\n- Herdr is a terminal workspace manager with a background server, workspaces, tabs and panes, agent state in a sidebar (`working`, `blocked`, `done`, `idle`, `unknown`), and the `Ctrl+B` prefix. The narrator's description matches. The official guide says new users need no keybindings.\n- Persistence matches: panes survive a closed window, and `herdr server stop` is the explicit stop.\n- Naming: the guide calls the top level a \"workspace\". The video says \"space\". Same thing, different wording.\n- Release state on 2026-10-10: the latest stable release is `v0.9.3`, published 2026-09-29, with a Windows build. `herdr update` installs it.\n- The video's claim that Cmux lacks persistence, and the tmux comparison, were not checked.\n\n## Limitations\n\n- Captions, not a script. Product names, tool comparisons and the phrase \"Fable\" in the demo should be checked against the video before quoting.\n- The video is a creator's endorsement with a sponsor link to his paid community. No benchmarks are given.\n- The demo skill (\"Codex review\") is the narrator's own and is not identified here.\n\n## How it fits this workspace\n\nStatus: **implemented and checked locally** for the install, not yet for a hands-on session. The working reference is [Claude and Codex on one project](../../references/claude-plus-codex.md), which holds the installed setup table, first-run commands and the shared-file guardrail. On 2026-10-10 that table was corrected after finding Herdr off the user PATH and no `codex` command resolving; Herdr is now `0.9.3`, Codex CLI `0.162.1` and Claude Code `2.1.295`. Details and open checks are on that page and in the [log](../log.md).\n\nRelated: [AI workspace hub](../../references/ai-workspace/index.md), [Claude plus Codex](../../references/claude-plus-codex.md).\n"}

## assistant

There's now a **Herdr** shortcut on your desktop at `C:\Users\Joshu\Desktop\Herdr.lnk`. Double-click it and Herdr opens in Windows Terminal, in your `ai-os` folder, with the Herdr icon.

I tested it by launching it the way a double-click would. Herdr started and attached to the running server. That test opened an extra Herdr window, which I closed. Your original window and the server are still running.

- **Update-proof:** the shortcut runs a small launcher at `C:\Users\Joshu\.herdr\launch-herdr.ps1`. The launcher finds the newest installed Herdr each time, so `herdr update` won't break it, and it doesn't rely on PATH. That PATH issue is what broke my first launch.
- **Two windows:** if Herdr is already running when you click it, you get a second window on the same session. Closing a window only detaches it. Your agents keep running.
- **Icon after updates:** the icon points at the 0.9.3 file. After a future update it may turn into a blank icon, though the shortcut will still work. Tell me and I'll repoint it.
- **Taskbar pin:** I made a desktop shortcut only. Windows doesn't allow scripts to pin to the taskbar. To pin it yourself, right-click the desktop icon and choose "Pin to taskbar".

10	
11	Codex model by role (Joshua, 2026-09-23):
12	- **Design, spec writing, plan review** (`/codex-review`, design critique): `gpt-6-astra` (he picked medium effort for plan review).
13	- **Building code** (`/codex-build`, any Codex implementation run): `gpt-6-luna` at `high` reasoning effort. Never astra.
14	- The config default (`gpt-6-luna`, low) is neither; always pass `-m` and `-c model_reasoning_effort=` explicitly and echo them before launch.
15	
16	- **Fixed 2026-09-23, path changed 2026-10-10:** a 400 "not supported when using Codex with a ChatGPT account" on sol/luna meant an outdated CLI, not the login. The fix then was 0.156.1 in `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\tools\node\codex`, but that directory no longer exists. On 2026-10-10 Codex CLI 0.162.1 was installed from npm into `AppData\Roaming\npm` and is the only `codex` on PATH (signed in with ChatGPT). Whether sol and luna run on 0.162.1 was not tested; if a 400 returns, check `codex --version` first. Joshua does not want an API-key login and picked `gpt-6-luna` high for builds (never luna low).
17	
18	**Why:** building from a frozen spec does not need the strongest model; astra's value is in thinking through design and specs. He objected when astra built Classroom Home phase 1 (PR #133).
19	
20	**How to apply:** on every Codex launch, choose the model by role, state it before running. Related: [[firecrawl-via-cli-not-mcp]] (same "use the cheaper path when it does the job" instinct).

1	---
2	name: powershell-msix-breaks-npm-clis
3	description: "The MSIX/Store build of PowerShell 7 cannot see AppData\\Roaming\\npm, silently breaking claude/codex/firecrawl/pnpm — install the MSI build instead."
4	metadata: 
5	  node_type: memory
6	  type: reference
7	  originSessionId: 0b4c747a-84ce-4095-8c2c-9bb1b7ec4ec8
8	  modified: 2026-09-07T22:58:04.061Z
9	---
10	
11	On Windows, `winget install Microsoft.PowerShell --scope user` installs the
12	**MSIX/Store** build (lands in `C:\Program Files\WindowsApps\...`). That build
13	runs with AppData virtualization and **cannot see `C:\Users\<user>\AppData\Roaming\npm`**.
14	
15	Consequence: `claude`, `codex`, `firecrawl`, and `pnpm` — every npm-global CLI —
16	fail to resolve in that shell, with no error explaining why. `Test-Path` on the
17	npm global dir returns `False` from MSIX pwsh and `True` from unpackaged
18	Windows PowerShell 5.1. That contrast is the fastest way to confirm the diagnosis.
19	
20	**Fix:** uninstall the MSIX package, then install the MSI from GitHub releases
21	(`PowerShell-<ver>-win-x64.msi`) via elevated `msiexec /i ... /qn /norestart`.
22	It installs to `C:\Program Files\PowerShell\7`, which is also the path Windows
23	Terminal's well-known PowerShell profile GUID
24	`{574e775e-4f2a-5b96-ac1e-a2962a402336}` expects.
25	
26	`winget --scope machine` refuses without admin, and `winget show` reports the
27	installer type as `msix` — so there is no winget-only path to the MSI.
28	
29	Verified 2026-09-07 on the Dell 15 DC15250 during [[ai-os-vault-location]] setup.
30

## assistant

Your setup is mostly right, and I fixed the one real problem I found: Codex, Firecrawl and Kling were invisible inside Herdr. The fix reaches your live Herdr session only after you restart it (steps below).

**Skills when you start from a terminal**
- **Claude:** started in your vault, it loads 376 skills, 7 agents and 11 plugins. Every skill your instructions name is there except the three below.
  - `claude-build` and `pre-consultation-check-in` are Codex-only by design (declared in `skills/PARITY.md`).
  - **`kling-cli` is the real gap.** PARITY says it's active for both, but its Claude copy is deleted in your working tree (uncommitted). The Codex copy is being edited at the same time, so another session looks mid-edit. I didn't restore it, because I'd be overwriting someone's work.
- **Codex:** it has 303 unique skills but only shows about 200. Its own warning says the skills budget was exceeded, it dropped all descriptions, and 321 skills were left out of its visible list. When I asked it for two hidden skills (`wrapup`, `herdr`), it found both by listing the folder. I did not prove it triggers hidden skills on its own.

**What I found and fixed**
- **Cause:** Windows Terminal is a Store app, and everything started under it, including Herdr, sees `AppData\Roaming\npm` as empty. Your memory already warned about this for PowerShell, and my Codex install repeated the mistake.
- **Fix:** I installed `codex` 0.162.1, `firecrawl-cli` 1.26.3 and `kling` 0.2.1 into `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\tools\node` and put that folder first on your user PATH. The old PATH is saved in the session scratchpad. The desktop shortcut's launcher now also rebuilds PATH from the registry on every start.
- **Test:** in a separate test session started through Windows Terminal, all 14 tools resolved inside a pane. Codex ran and showed ChatGPT sign-in, and Claude 2.1.295 ran. The skill folders and the vault were readable. I stopped that test session afterward.

**Needs one step from you**
Your live Herdr session (with its two Claude agents) still has the old PATH, and I didn't touch it. Run `herdr server stop` when you're at a good stopping point, then reopen from the desktop shortcut.

**Not fixed or not checked**
- **Validator:** `check-skills.sh` couldn't run, because `python` on this laptop is the Microsoft Store stub (`py` works).
- **Firecrawl:** `--status` says "Not authenticated" both inside and outside Herdr, though scrapes worked earlier. I didn't investigate.
- **Duplicate copies:** the old copies in `AppData\Roaming\npm` remain, and nothing was deleted.
- **Notes updated:** the vault reference, a log entry and two of your memory notes are updated.

## Current AI OS execution contract

Use the host-supported time/file tools, preserve current open state and explicit scope. Chat-only continuity remains distinct from file-writing wrapup or a broad system audit.

# Session Handoff

Produce a repeatable end-of-session summary so Joshua can `/clear` and start a fresh agent without losing continuity. The next agent should pick up by reading this summary alone.

This is a **context-handoff artifact**, not a status report. The audience is a future instance of you, not a stakeholder.

**Handoff vs. wrapup:** run `session-handoff` when the *context window* is the problem — nearing ~250k tokens (the dumb zone), or passing work between Claude and Codex. It is chat-only and writes nothing. Run `wrapup` when the *work day* is done — it strikes tasks, refreshes `wiki/hot.md`, and appends `wiki/log.md`. They can be run back to back (wrapup to persist, then handoff to reset).

## When to invoke

Joshua says: "session handoff", "hand off", "handoff summary", "summarize before I clear", or any near-equivalent. Note "let's wrap up" belongs to `wrapup`, not here — if he says that and the context window is also deep, run `wrapup` first, then offer this. Also invoke proactively if he says he's about to `/clear` without having run it, or if the session is clearly deep into the dumb zone.

## How to produce the summary

1. **Review the full conversation**, not just the last few turns. Handoffs miss things when they only summarize recent context.
2. **Pull state from these sources (in order):**
   - Plan files referenced this session (check `~/.claude/plans/` if a plan was mentioned).
   - TodoWrite state — any in-progress or pending tasks.
   - Background processes started with `run_in_background` — shell IDs are load-bearing for the next agent.
   - Files created or modified this session — you know what you touched; don't grep to re-discover.
   - Memory files written or updated (`~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/`).
   - Unresolved questions — things you asked Joshua that never got a clear answer, or things he asked that got deflected.
3. **Do NOT audit the filesystem.** This is synthesis of what happened in THIS session. No `git log`, no broad `Glob` sweeps. If you didn't touch it this session, it doesn't belong here.
4. **Produce the output in chat.** Do not write a file. Do not update memory. Chat-only.

## Output template — use exactly this structure, every time

```
# Session Handoff — <one-line title of what this session was about>

## Where it started
<2-3 sentences: what Joshua asked for, key framing or constraints that emerged>

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- <decision or change> — <why, and where it lives (absolute path if a file)>
- ...

## Key files for next session
- `<absolute path>` — <why the next agent should read this first>
- Plan file: `<path>` (if a plan drove the session)
- Memory files touched: `<paths>` (if any)

## Running state
- Background processes: <shell IDs + what they are + how to kill> — or "none"
- Dev servers / ports: <url + port> — or "none"
- Open worktrees / branches: <paths> — or "none"

## Verified state as of <HH:MM>
- <claim about live/system state> — <how it was checked, and when>
- <claim> — NOT re-verified this session; last confirmed <when>
- ...

## Verification — how to confirm things still work
- `<command>` — <expected outcome>
- ...

## Deferred + open questions
- Deferred: <item> — <why pushed to later>
- OPEN (must be resolved or re-asked before new work): <question needing Joshua's input> — <context>

## Pick up here
<1-2 sentences: the single most likely next action for a fresh agent>
```

## Hard rules

1. **Chat output only.** Never write the handoff to a file. Never update memory from this skill. (That is `wrapup`'s job, not this one.)
2. **Never invent state.** If a section has nothing to report, write "none" — do not omit the section. Structure stability is the whole point.
3. **Absolute paths always.** The next agent may have a different working directory, or be Codex.
4. **If a plan file drove the session, name it first** in "Key files" so the next agent reads it before anything else.
5. **No emojis, no hype, no "great job" summaries.** Terse and concrete — paths, commands, shell IDs, decisions. Tone of a seasoned engineer handing off at end-of-shift.
6. **Background process IDs are critical.** If you started any `run_in_background` shells, their IDs must appear in "Running state" with the kill command — the next agent cannot find them otherwise.
7. **Stamp verified state, and separate it from assumed state.** Run `date "+%H:%M"` and use the real time. Every claim about live systems (deploy status, DB contents, what was sent, what is published) is either something you actually checked this session — say how — or explicitly marked NOT re-verified. A handoff that presents stale belief as current state makes the next session re-derive everything, which is the exact cost this skill exists to remove.
8. **Open questions are obligations, not notes.** Anything under OPEN must be resolved by the next session or re-asked to Joshua before it starts new work. Requirements have been silently re-typed across sessions, and a promised artifact once never arrived because nobody re-asked. If a question was open in the handoff you are picking up from, it is the first thing you raise.

## Anti-patterns — do not do these

- Summarizing the last 3 turns and calling it a handoff.
- Listing files by relative path.
- Skipping "Running state" because "nothing is running" — write "none" instead.
- Writing the summary to any file. This is chat-only by design.
- Adding a "what went well / what went poorly" retrospective. This isn't a retro.
- Recommending next steps beyond the single "Pick up here" line. The next agent decides; you just hand off.
